=== review:cppguide
SUMMARY: Short version: I reviewed the added C++ of PR-4 (the fourth and last pull request of the tron PR 4424 split; tron is the inference program) against the cpp-coding-guide skill and the lens list. No must-fix. Three should-fix items (a needless narrowing to uint32_t in two edits, and one unused include) and eight nits, six of them report-only.

Words used here: the option = the CMake switch TRON_K_VNNI (the pair-interleaved K cache layout). The diff = `git diff jhan-vnnik-3-shared-save HEAD` in /home/jhan/workspace/ai-runs/tron-split-pr4 (HEAD 144a6dedd3). Lint = the CI job that runs clang-format (ColumnLimit 88, ReflowComments true, .clang-format:85 and :168).

Guide rules checked (the skill has two rule groups):
- Named values. Every new fixed value is a constexpr whose name carries the value: PANEL_ROWS_4, BLOCKS_PER_PAGE_MASK_0XF (tensor/k_vnni.hpp:92-94, with static_asserts at 108-113), EAGLE_STORAGE_SLOTS_1, BLOCK_COMPLETED_FALSE, CONVERTED_FALSE/TRUE (kv_cache.hpp:2498-2504), REPEAT_FALSE, AMX_AVAILABLE_*, RECORD_ROWS_* (t_amx_dispatch_dtype.cpp:79-83), SEED_23, ROW_BYTES_256, NO_BLOCK_0X0, NEXT_ROW_1, SUPPORT_EAGLE_TRUE, N_SLOTS_2, SLOT_1, SAVED_TOKENS_21, LAST_OF_BLOCK_0_15, FORGOTTEN_TOKEN_*, BLOCK_0_ONLY_0X1, BLOCKS_0_AND_1_0X3, OLD/NEW_TOKENS_*, DST_SIZE_20, SRC_TOKENS_10, COPIED_END_30, X_BYTES_16, STORAGE_SLOTS_2 (t_k_vnni_layout.cpp), each with a static_assert where the value is computed. The literal `1` inside the named helpers block_bit and offset_bit is accepted (the name carries the meaning). Deviations: the `bit(n) - 1` idiom (cppguide-11, nit), `return true` (cppguide-6, nit), the lower-case names of t_llama_unit.cpp, which match that file (cppguide-7, nit). `alignas(k_vnni::LINE_BYTES_64)` uses the shared constant, which the guide allows.
- Braces. Every multi-line control-flow body in the added code has braces. The brace-less bodies are all single physical lines (kv_cache.hpp:2286, 2342, 2561; model.hpp:3177; t_k_vnni_layout.cpp:652; t_llama_unit.cpp pre_save and check loops), which the guide allows.

Lens items checked:
- Includes. tensor/k_vnni.hpp adds <cstring> for the memcpy of store_row_major/load_row_major; <type_traits> was present for std::conditional_t. kv_cache.hpp has <atomic> and <array>. self_attention.hpp has <optional> (line 11). model.hpp has <vector>. t_k_vnni_layout.cpp adds <cstdlib> for std::abs on a pointer difference. full.hpp needs nothing new. One unused include found (cppguide-2).
- noexcept and attributes against neighbours. row_major_row `TRON(nodiscard, inline, pure)` matches at() and plane() (tensor/k_vnni.hpp:181, 242); store_row_major, load_row_major, block_to_vnni, block_to_rows `TRON(inline) ... noexcept` match store_row/load_row/store_block/copy_token (352-435); page::k_vnni_blocks and note_k_row_saved `TRON(nodiscard, inline)` without `pure` (they read atomics) match is_kv_complete (kv_cache.hpp:2465); k_row_if_row_major carries TRON(this_lifetimebound) like k() (2046); k_with_storage_geometry (no noexcept, no attribute) matches book::for_each_active_slot (1699-1700); k_forget_row and clear_kv_complete_at are plain noexcept; block_bit/offset_bit are plain constexpr like k_vnni::index. No deviation.
- Naming and const. Short names (pg, c, t, cbit, state_ix, storage) follow the files; `const size_t c = ..., t = ...;` (tensor/k_vnni.hpp:475) matches lines 292 and 298 of the same file. Locals are const where not reassigned. `const bool vnni_build` could be constexpr (cppguide-8, nit).
- Narrowing casts. `static_cast<uint32_t>(j / k_store_block)` has no purpose (cppguide-1a/1b). `uint8_t(k_vnni::block_bit(c) - 1)` and `uint8_t(~k_vnni::block_bit(c))` (kv_cache.hpp:2557, 2746) are required by integer promotion and use the functional-cast form of PR-3's `__mmask16(1u << ...)` (model.hpp:3296); `static_cast<size_t>(pg.offset_in_page(...))` matches model.hpp:3222 and 3282.
- [[maybe_unused]]. The five in copy_storage_slot (kv_cache.hpp:2699-2705) and the two new ones in self_attention.hpp (1982-1983, `vnni_blocks` and `plane`) are the same pattern as the pre-existing `live` and `keys` placeholders (1975, 1994): in the row-major instantiation the variables appear only inside discarded `if constexpr` branches. `(void)slot` (kv_cache.hpp:2172) matches `(void)max_model_layers` (880) and full.hpp's `(void)dest`. One superfluous attribute found (cppguide-3). The two unused test constants in the dispatch-off build are D12 (cppguide-10, report only).
- std::optional in the hot loop (self_attention.hpp:1983-1990). k_vnni_view has no default constructor and no public pointer constructor (tensor/k_vnni.hpp:160-200: that is the type's stated contract), so an optional is the only way to defer the fetch to the first relevant token without changing PR-1's type. The per-token cost is one engaged-flag test (`if (!plane)`, line 2019) at the same place where the row-major loop tests `keys.valid()` (2033), plus one dereference per relevant tail row and one for the reader call after the loop (2047). Accepted; the fetch at the first relevant token keeps the rule that a page nothing is relevant for is never asked for its state.
- The 4 KiB stack arrays. block_to_vnni holds `bf16 rows[16][128]` (4096 bytes) plus 16 row pointers (128 bytes); block_to_rows holds the 4096-byte array (tensor/k_vnni.hpp:508-527). Callers: convert_pending_k_blocks (main thread, forward end, model.hpp:3176-3183), k_forget_row via clear_kv_complete_at (the scheduler, tree lock held, kv_cache.hpp:2554-2588) and copy_storage_slot via book::append (the scheduler, full.hpp:1439). The calls are sequential, never nested, so the peak is one 4 KiB frame per call. store_k_block already keeps a 4 KiB `conv[16][128]` on the main and helper threads (model.hpp:3272, PR-3). No production thread sets a stack size; the only reduced stack in the tree is a test's own 512 KiB thread (t/t_rinzler_proxy.cpp:2345). Accepted.
- Line length and formatting. No added line exceeds 88 columns (awk over the diff). I ran clang-format 19.1.7 (via `uvx --from clang-format==19.1.7`, the same version the handoff names) on scratch copies of the five files with my edits applied: the only difference from clang-format's output was the line break of cppguide-1b, which the proposed text already uses. So the HEAD files are Lint-clean and every proposed edit keeps them so. The worktree was not modified.
- Other compile-time checks made while reading: TRON_ASSERT always evaluates its condition (common/assert.hpp:69-74), so `present` in store_k_block stays used after the masked path's removal; book befriends page (kv_cache.hpp:1417), so k_storage_readable may read reclaimable_kv_restore_attempt_; the staging lambda in full.hpp has an explicit `-> bf16*` return type, so returning `row.data` and `dest` is well-formed; k_vnni_view's writable-to-const converting constructor (tensor/k_vnni.hpp:173-177) makes `const k_vnni_view<const bf16, ...> source = plane;` valid; k_row_view default-constructs to nullptr and has valid() (view.hpp:296, 316).

Not covered by this lens: the layout-state protocol, the conversion ordering, the hardware-attention policy and the test logic (other lenses). Not run: any build or test (no nix on this host; the machine delphi-3bda was not touched).

=== review:tests
SUMMARY: Short version. I read the whole PR-4 diff (12 files) and the three test files at HEAD 144a6dedd3 against the production code they drive, and found no must-fix: no test that cannot fail, no REQUIRE on the wrong object, no poison or seed collision, and both mutation predictions of the handoff hold by reading. I report 4 should-fix items (a wrong setup comment, an overstated comment about the hardware-attention sections, an untested accessor the FPGA staging reads, and a re-save check that cannot see the written value) and 8 nits.

Words used here: VNNI slot / row-major block / converted block / saved-row bits as in PR4-handoff.md "Words used here"; "the option" = TRON_K_VNNI; "the page-state case" = the t_k_vnni_layout case "page K blocks turn VNNI at the 16th saved row and back when a row is forgotten"; M1 / M2 = the two mutation checks of handoff step 6.

(1) What each test proves and what breaks it.
- t_k_vnni_layout, case "row-major tail block converts ... bit for bit": section 1 proves that row_major_row rows are 64-byte aligned, lie inside panel ((t/4)*4 + c) of their own block, and do not overlap (every pair at least 256 bytes apart). Section 2 proves that block_to_vnni of 16 row-major rows equals store_row per token over the whole 16 KiB plane (memcmp), that block_to_rows inverts it (the 16 rows read back through row_major_row), and that neither conversion touches another block (poison 0xa5a5 in the other 48 tokens). Fails on a wrong panel address, a wrong transpose, or a store that spills out of the block. Works on an owner, so it runs the same in every build.
- The page-state case proves the transitions of Note [K VNNI storage]: the block bit appears exactly at the 16th saved row (REQUIRE on save_row_state and vnni_blocks per row), rows stay readable through get_k_row across conversion, forget (clear_kv_complete_at 3, 18, 16, 3), re-save and re-conversion; the plane holds VNNI columns for block 0 and row-major rows for block 1 (the #ifdef block); forgetting offset 3 converts block 0 back (rows 0..2 readable) while block 1's 4 panels stay byte-identical (the snapshot). Fails on M1, on k_forget_row not converting back, on wrong bits dropped, on set_k_row choosing the layout wrongly.
- "the EAGLE storage view keeps its own K layout state": the bits and the conversion follow the view (k_state_ix, k_storage_of); fails if the entry or the storage were shared.
- "forgetting a row acts on every slot's own block": two slots, converted in slot 1 only; fails if k_forget_row transposed slot 0's row-major bytes or missed slot 1's bits.
- "page append carries the K layout state with the rows": token-by-token append into a partly filled page (bits 0..44, blocks 0 and 1 converted), append into a converted destination block (block 1 goes back to rows, keeps rows 16..19 and 30..31, comes back VNNI), whole-page append (0xf copied). Fails if copy_storage_slot dropped or misplaced the bits or copied through the wrong layout.
- t_llama_unit, the K-instance block of check_page_range_reductions: gives the VNNI build the row-major build's K values (the inverse index map reads the raw bytes as linear rows, set_k_row writes them into the row-major block placement, which differs from the linear placement for t >= 4), then page 0 dense and every later page mixed (blocks 0 and 1 VNNI, a 9-row tail, block 3 row-major unrecorded); the attention result is compared with the emulation reference. Proves the dense gate plus the mixed software loop (reader for VNNI blocks, dotters for tail rows) against one reference. Fails if the loop missed a token or scored one through the wrong layout.
- The save_k case: pre_save marks rows 0..4 and 8 so that the 15-token run 5..15 (token 8 has no KV work and does not end the run, model.hpp:3281-3283) completes block 0 through save_k; block 3 is a whole run (set_k_block + note_k_block_saved). check_blocks(false) = 0x8 then convert_pending_k_blocks then 0x9 proves the forward-end queue; the second check(before) proves the conversion is value-preserving, also for the pre-marked rows 0..4 and 8 that no job stored. check_marks proves one K mark per job with KV work. The "scores the operation" section proves no bit and no conversion for a hardware-scored operation while the whole-block save still writes VNNI (0x8 twice). The "another operation" section proves the flag is per operation (see tests-3 for what it does not prove).
- t_amx_dispatch_dtype: exercise_page now records rows like save_k (note_k_row_saved + k_block_to_vnni), so the full page is 0xf and the existing dense expectations hold under the option; the new section stores a full page without recording and expects one pack, no kernel, one AVX visit of 64 tokens under the option. Fails if the third gate (k_layout_dense) is missing.

(2) Mutation predictions. M1 holds: with `!=`, at tok = 0 `after` is bit 0, the block test is true and the block bit is clear, so note_k_row_saved returns true; save_row_state then calls k_block_to_vnni, which refuses (rows incomplete) and the REQUIRE at t/t_k_vnni_layout.cpp:691 (macro line; the expression is on 692) compares true with false under CAPTURE(tok) = 0; nothing before it asserts. With the option off the mutated line is never reached. M2 holds: cached_operation_uses_hw is `attention_operation_uses_hw[operation.i] != 0`; the state is fresh per section with the array zeroed. One-thread, another-operation (flag set on index 1 only) and three-thread (window 1): the mutant records nothing, the queue is empty, check_blocks(true) fails at t/t_llama_unit.cpp:4054 with 0x8 against 0x9. "Scores the operation": the mutant records, block 0 is queued, the first check_blocks(false) passes (0x8, not converted yet), convert_pending_k_blocks converts, the second check_blocks(false) fails with 0x9 against 0x8. Four failed assertions, one failed test case; the helpers of the three-thread window are joined inside shared_save before the failing REQUIRE. Catch2 prints the uint8_t values as `8` and `'\t'` (tests-11).

(3) Three-thread section. Both windows carry pre_save, snapshot, shared_save, check, check_marks, check_blocks(false), convert_pending_k_blocks, check_blocks(true), check, check_window; PR-3's late helper (20 ms) and the finished count read at return are kept (the diff adds lines only in that section). The second window uses active_attention_operations[1] (layer 1, KV slot 1) and reassigns `slot`, which every lambda reads by reference. Isolation: k_rows_saved_ and k_vnni_blocks_ are indexed by k_state_ix(slot) = slot.i (no EAGLE view), kv_saves_ by [offset][slot.i], the pending list is main-only and cleared by convert_pending_k_blocks (a stale entry would re-run k_block_to_vnni_at on a converted block of slot 0, a no-op invisible to the slot-1 checks), and the window counters are the batch's, which check_window(2, ...) accounts for. Nothing of window 1 can make window 2's checks pass or fail wrongly.

(4) With the option off: the conversion case runs unchanged (owner only). The page-state, EAGLE, forget and append cases run with save_row_state always false and vnni_blocks always 0, so they check row survival through set/get/forget/append with the #ifdef plane checks compiled out. In the save_k case check_blocks is empty, note_k_row_saved returns false, and the two hardware-attention sections repeat the one-thread section's checks (tests-12). The dispatch test's new section equals section 1's single round with the option off; with AMX dispatch off the whole case is the SUCCEED placeholder and the two file-scope constants give the two expected warnings (D12, not fixed).

(5) The "page 1 up to row 40" comment: confirmed wrong for the uses_hw instantiation (10 pages); exact fix in tests-1, name in tests-2.

(6) Gaps found: k_row_if_row_major untested anywhere (tests-4); the re-save into a converted block cannot see the written value and check_rows's skip parameter is dead (tests-5); k_block_to_vnni's refusal on a converted block and note_k_row_saved's "true again" on a complete unconverted block are unasserted (tests-6). Checked and found no problem: seeds 7, 19, 23, 31 are distinct within t_k_vnni_layout; the fill_row salts of one case fall in distinct ranges (salt % 4); poison 0xa5a5 is finite and only compared bit-exactly; every REQUIRE reads the page, slot and view it means to; the pre_save rows, the no-KV token 8 and the run grouping agree with store_k_block's run rule; the append expectations agree with copy_storage_slot's bit arithmetic (range, c_begin, dst_end); attention_op_id's `!=` is the C++20 rewrite of its operator==; all proposed old texts occur exactly once at HEAD (grep -c). Every proposed edit is test or comment text, compiles in the three configurations by reading (the new checks are either under #ifdef TRON_K_VNNI or valid with the bits at 0), and should be run through the step-4.2 clang-format dry run by the executor.

=== review:items
SUMMARY: Lens: the ten reviewer items of PR4-handoff.md step 5.2, read at HEAD 144a6dedd3 of /home/jhan/workspace/ai-runs/tron-split-pr4 (read-only; no build, no git state change, no machine).

Result: 1 must-fix (a false comment claim: page::v_ptr does not exist, kv_cache.hpp:801), 2 should-fix (a commit-message claim that matches no hunk of its commit, b.txt; the "page 1" comment of t_llama_unit.cpp:2508), 6 nits (two of them report-only: the D12 warnings and the option-off duplicate coverage). No wrong behaviour, crash or build failure found in the three configurations by reading.

Item by item:
(1) Layout-state protocol: PASS. Bits are per storage slot with one extra entry for the EAGLE storage (kv_cache.hpp:2612-2623, k_state_ix :2503-2507). note_k_row_saved sets the row bit and returns true only when the block's 16 bits are set and the block bit is clear (:2169-2187), so a re-saved row of a complete unconverted block returns true again and a row of a converted block returns false, as its comment says. note_k_block_saved sets 16 row bits then the block bit (:2196-2203). Clears: k_forget_row via clear_kv_complete_at (:2474-2478, :2554-2600) drops bits at and above the offset in every slot and converts the offset's block back first when it is converted, keeps rows and the storage is readable; copy_storage_slot replaces the destination range's row bits by the source's and recomputes block bits (:2693-2786). Every writer and reader takes the layout from the block bit: set_k_row :2254-2260, get_k_row :2299-2304, k_row_if_row_major :2344-2351, k_block_to_vnni_at :2225-2246, the dense gate self_attention.hpp:1926, the software loop :2021-2027, the staging full.hpp:2762-2767; the page move constructor copies both arrays (:1970-1975). I also checked the KV-restore path: invalidate_restored_book (full.hpp:3330-3350) clears bits while the storage is unreadable (restore attempt != 0) and marks every entry for recomputation, so no live row is read from unconverted bytes.
(2) Conversion at the forward end: PASS. convert_pending_k_blocks runs after wait_for_coop_gof and before the logit launch (model.hpp:1838-1851; run_forward is the only forward path, :1769). The record loop runs after the join on main only: helpers return at :3365 and :3396-3399 before it (:3419-3437). The storage ref is taken at the save (`pg.k_storage_of(slot)`, :3433). The queue is drained in the same pass, so no stale page pointer crosses a tree mutation.
(3) Hardware-attention policy: PASS. save_k_impl reads cached_operation_uses_hw(binding.operation) with binding = operation.binding (:3366, :3425); the whole-block path (:3323-3339) is outside that predicate, so a 16-token run still writes VNNI and sets the block bit; the staging returns the row in place for a row-major block and gathers with get_k_row otherwise (full.hpp:2762-2767). The test sections distinguish the saving operation from operation 1 (t_llama_unit.cpp:4096-4106).
(4) Dense gate: PASS. k_layout_dense is the third && operand after amx_q_packed and is_dense_amx_page (self_attention.hpp:1924-1937), so a page with no relevant token is never asked; the software loop fetches the block mask and the plane at the first relevant token (:2019-2022), sets live only for converted blocks (:2024-2025), scores tail rows with the dotters through row_major_row (:2027-2031; the dotter's const_view overload at dotter.hpp:186-189 takes the 64-byte-aligned row), and calls qk_group only when live != 0 (:2045-2049). <optional> is included (:11).
(5) block_to_vnni / block_to_rows: PASS. row_major_row addresses panel ((t / 4) * 4 + c) at row (t % 4) * 256 bytes (tensor/k_vnni.hpp:470-480): every offset is a multiple of 64 bytes on the 64-byte-aligned owner (:210-231), rows of one block are distinct and inside its own 4 panels. block_to_vnni copies the 16 rows to an aligned stack array and calls store_block with FULL_BLOCK_0XFFFF (:503-517), which the PR-3 test proved bit-identical to store_row; block_to_rows is the inverse (:520-533). The test case at t_k_vnni_layout.cpp:268-339 checks alignment, distinctness, panel membership, bit identity per block, the inverse and untouched other blocks (poison).
(6) Copy and append: PASS. Whole page: owner assignment plus both bit sets (:2740-2742, :2719-2721). Partial range: touched converted destination blocks go row-major, rows are copied through row-major rows, blocks complete afterwards go VNNI (:2743-2771); bits stored after the last head (:2782-2785). The EAGLE storage gets its own k_storage_ref (:2803-2805). The three append sections (t_k_vnni_layout.cpp:906-1031) and the EAGLE case (:758-834) cover the cases the handoff names; I traced the expected masks 0x3, 0xF, 0xF by hand and they match.
(7) Removed masked path: PASS. `if (n < k_store_block)` stores row by row (model.hpp:3303-3322); the else branch asserts present == FULL_BLOCK_0XFFFF, calls set_k_block without a mask and note_k_block_saved (:3323-3339); set_k_block has no mask parameter (kv_cache.hpp:2278-2286) and store_block keeps `present` (tensor/k_vnni.hpp:404-409).
(8) Tests: PASS with one comment fix (lens10-3). The five tail-block cases (t_k_vnni_layout.cpp:268, 650, 758, 836, 870) prove, in order: conversion identity and locality; the VNNI transition at the 16th row, the reverse transition on a forget, readability across every transition and untouched bytes of blocks above; the EAGLE view's own state; per-slot forgetting; append state. The dispatch test records rows like save_k and adds the unrecorded-full-page section (t_amx_dispatch_dtype.cpp:131-137, :321-325). Every save_k section checks marks and block masks before and after the conversion (t_llama_unit.cpp:4068-4073, :4089-4094, :4109-4114, :4214-4220, :4237-4243); I traced the expected masks 0x8 then 0x9 for page 0 from the batch (:3901-3920) and the pre-saved rows (:4035-4048). With the option off: the page-state cases run with vnni_build false and expect no block, the k_vnni_blocks calls are under #ifdef, the K-instance block of t_llama_unit is #ifdef TRON_K_VNNI, check_blocks is a no-op, and the dispatch test's dense predicate falls back to !layout_on.
(9) Unused constants: report only (lens10-8), decision D12.
(10) Three-thread section: PASS. The late helper (:4127-4133) and finished_at_return (:4139) are kept; both windows carry check_marks, check_blocks(false), convert_pending_k_blocks, check_blocks(true) and a second check (:4214-4220, :4237-4243); check_window(1, finished_0) and check_window(2, finished_1) read the count taken at return (:4222, :4245).

=== review:protocol
SUMMARY: Short version: I read the PR-4 diff (jhan-vnnik-3-shared-save..HEAD at /home/jhan/workspace/ai-runs/tron-split-pr4) and the code around it (model.hpp run_forward and save_k_impl, full.hpp page_info/k_head_fn, the three clear_kv_complete_at callers, _defrag_books/_relocate, gof.hpp/gof.cpp populate and backfill, token_tree.hpp, token_cache.cpp oom_rescue, src/tron/scheduler/full.cpp restore sequence, common.hpp Notes). I found one wrong-behaviour defect (protocol-1: k_storage_readable checks the slot's first chunk, so a sliding-window slot can drop a converted block's bit without transposing it back) and one false claim in the Note (protocol-2: the attention workers of an earlier minibatch do read a block while a later minibatch saves it). Everything else in the layout-state protocol held up under every interleaving I could construct.

Interleavings tried and found safe:
- (a) Shared window: a helper's note_k_block_saved (rows fetch_or acq_rel, then the block bit fetch_or release, both after the loop over every KV head) is ordered before main's note_k_row_saved loop by finished.fetch_add(release) / main's acquire spin. Main's loop over all items then sees the 16 row bits set (skips the fetch_or) and the block bit set (returns false, so no spurious queue). No row bit can be lost: the only setters are fetch_or, and the only clearers (k_forget_row fetch_and, copy_storage_slot store) run under the tree lock between passes. No reader can see the block bit before every head holds the layout: every reader (set_k_row, get_k_row, k_row_if_row_major, the dense gate, the software loop's first-relevant-token fetch, copy_storage_slot) loads the bit with acquire. A whole-block save and a partial save of the same block cannot coexist in one window (16 offsets, duplicate offsets end a run and are not produced by the scheduler).
- (b) Readers between save_k and the forward end: attention workers of the same pass take the layout from k_vnni_blocks (acquire) and the bits of a short-run block do not change in-pass; a whole-block save publishes before the downstream latch count_down. The coop gather: help_drain_gof -> fire_gof_rodeo -> gof_rodeo_impl -> prepare_for_dma -> populate (src/tron/gof.cpp 188-222) calls k_head_fn per head and copies the row into the staging block (k.set_position) inside the same call; the in-place pointer from k_row_if_row_major is never retained. wait_for_coop_gof drains the queue and spins on gof_coop_outstanding_ == 0 before convert_pending_k_blocks, and run() joined the workers before that. The FPGA DMA reads the staging buffer only. The lazy populate of backfill_shard runs in compute_forward_args under the tree lock before a forward. A GOF of tokens inside a whole-block-saved block cannot be complete before that save (its tokens are part of the run).
- (c) clear_kv_complete_at callers: create_child_at_location (full.hpp 669) and extend_leaf_edge (818) run from _add_tokens_locked on the worker under tree_lock in the request step that completes before run_executors->forward(); invalidate_restored_book (3346) runs inside commit_reclaimed_kv_restore in the same locked step (src/tron/scheduler/full.cpp 128-176). With a restore in flight k_forget_row drops bits only, and the commit invalidates every entry of the chain so every row is replayed. The EAGLE entry (index n_slots) is handled by the eagle/has_eagle_storage checks; k_storage_of resolves the view at the save and k_block_to_vnni_at uses the physical storage, so the reset_storage_view after the EAGLE forward (full.hpp 2100) cannot redirect a conversion. The defect of protocol-1 is in the readability test for chunked (sliding-window) slots.
- (d) copy_storage_slot: range = ~0 only when count == page_size (which forces src_begin == dst_begin == 0, the whole_page path); otherwise (offset_bit(count) - 1) << dst_begin; new_saved replaces exactly the destination range with the shifted source bits. The partial path converts every touched converted destination block back first (rows outside the range keep their row-major form and their bits), copies row by row through row-major rows, and re-converts a block whose 16 bits are set after the copy. Stale bits above the book's token count (after a decrement) are tolerated and dropped by the next k_forget_row at that offset. The whole-page path copies bytes and both bit sets.
- (e) Dense gate: the conversion runs after run() returned (the plugin joins its main helpers and attention workers, Note [Plugin-owned worker launch]); the logit-delivery workers and the matmul completion callbacks (Note [Matrix callback lifetime]) read no K page. A worker of the same pass therefore cannot see a converted bit for row-major bytes; the next pass's workers are ordered by the pool enqueue.
- (f) note_k_row_saved: relaxed load then acq_rel fetch_or; the forward-end reader is the same thread; helpers never read row bits.
- (g) Pending list: run_forward is noexcept and has no return between plugin_state.run and convert_pending_k_blocks; the scheduler's early return (token_jobs.empty) precedes the model forward; a pass without listeners still reaches the conversion. save_k's main side runs on the thread that called forward() in the generated plugins (TronCpp.hs 1364-1370: run() calls run_main_help on helpers only) and in h/tron/plugins/llama.hpp (rope_k from run()); helpers return before the note loop. Book relocation, defrag and free are serialized by tree_lock on the worker between forwards; the listener-driven ~node -> _defrag_books on the app pool runs during logit delivery, after convert_pending_k_blocks. The off-thread OOM rescue (token_cache.cpp 233-250) can evict under tree_lock while a forward is dispatched; a sibling defrag there would move pages under the running forward's token_pages, which is a pre-existing reliance that the Note names ("shares this reliance with the book relocation it already does") and PR-4 does not widen (the pending list's page pointers live no longer than token_pages).

Not checked by this lens: the test content for cannot-fail cases, plain-English wording outside the two cited paragraphs, the dispatch test's unused constants (decision D12, report only, no finding), and build warnings (no compiler on this host).

- [should-fix] cppguide-1a (cppguide) h/tron/models/model.hpp:3170 pending_k_block::block is uint32_t although its producer and its consumer are size_t, so the value is narrowed at the push and widened again at the conversion for no gain.
  detail: The field `uint32_t block` (model.hpp:3170) receives `static_cast<uint32_t>(j / k_store_block)` (model.hpp:3434; `j` is a size_t page offset below 64) and is passed to `page::k_block_to_vnni_at(k_storage_ref storage, size_t c)` (kv_cache.hpp:2321-2322). The narrowing buys no space: `k_storage_ref` is two size_t fields (kv_storage_offset holds a `size_t i`, common.hpp:242-243; `state_ix` is size_t), so the struct is 8 + 16 + 4 = 28 bytes padded to 32 bytes with uint32_t and 8 + 16 + 8 = 32 bytes with size_t. A reviewer who reads a narrowing cast expects a reason (a packed shared struct, as the window's `unit_start` uint32_t at model.hpp:3225) and finds none. This edit and cppguide-1b must be applied together: 1b alone makes the braced initializer a narrowing size_t-to-uint32_t conversion, which is ill-formed in list-initialization. Applying only 1a compiles.
  fix: Apply together with cppguide-1b. Exact old text (once at HEAD, lines 3169-3171):
      typename page::k_storage_ref storage;
      uint32_t block;
    };
Exact new text:
      typename page::k_storage_ref storage;
      size_t block;
    };

  vote correctness: stands: Not refuted. Every substantive claim in the finding matches the code at HEAD 144a6dedd3; the only error is a citation line number.

Verified facts (file:line at /home/jhan/workspace/ai-runs/tron-split-pr4):
- The field is `uint32_t block` [h/tron/models/model.hpp:3170].
- The producer is `const size_t j = static_cast<size_t>(pg.offset_in_page(tok_ix));` then `pending_k_blocks_.push_back({&pg, pg.k_storage_of(slot), static_cast<uint32_t>(j / k_store_block)});` [model.hpp:3431-3434]. `j` is below page_size 64 (kv_cache.hpp:1944), so the block index is 0..3 and fits any width.
- The consumer is `bool k_block_to_vnni_at(k_storage_ref storage, size_t c)` [h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp:2216], called as `b.pg->k_block_to_vnni_at(b.storage, b.block)` [model.hpp:3180]. The finding cites kv_cache.hpp:2321-2322 for this; the correct line is 2216. The signature it quotes is right. Everything downstream is size_t too: `block_bit(size_t c)` [h/tron/tensor/k_vnni.hpp:98], `TRON_ASSERT_LT(c, page_size / k_vnni::BLOCK_TOKENS_16)` [kv_cache.hpp:2217].
- `k_storage_ref` is `kv_storage_offset offset; size_t state_ix;` [kv_cache.hpp:2147-2150] and `kv_storage_offset` holds one `size_t i` [h/tron/models/common.hpp:242-243]. A standalone g++ -std=c++20 check of the two layouts (scratchpad sz.cpp) prints `k_storage_ref=16 uint32_t_variant=32 size_t_variant=32`. The narrowing saves no bytes, as the finding says.
- `block` has no other reader or writer: grep over h/, src/, t/ shows only the definition (3170), the push (3433-3434) and the consumer call (3180). Tests call `state.convert_pending_k_blocks()` only [t/t_llama_unit.cpp:4071,4093,4113,4218,4241].
- Inside the same save_k function, the same block index computed for the store window is `size_t run_block` and `const size_t block = ... / k_store_block` [model.hpp:3215, 3221-3223], so the uint32_t field is inconsistent with its own function.
- No stated reason exists for the width: the commit that added the field (234a238c65f) does not mention it in its message; the cpp-coding-guide skill (~/.claude/skills/cpp-coding-guide/SKILL.md) has no integer-width rule; the 4424 reference worktree (tron-split-probe-pr4ref, c4bf86005a, model.hpp:3164-3168) carries the identical `uint32_t block`, so the width was inherited, not chosen for this struct.
- The contrast with `unit_start` holds: that is `std::array<uint32_t, max_minibatch_size + 1> unit_start` inside the cross-thread `k_store_window_t` [common.hpp, k_store_window_t], where the array width is a real size concern.
- The finding's claim that 1a alone compiles is correct: list-initializing a size_t member from a uint32_t expression is a widening conversion.

The finding describes a readability issue, not a runtime defect, which matches its should-fix label. Nothing in the code shows the narrowing has a purpose, so the finding stands.

Apply it with cppguide-1b so the push at model.hpp:3434 becomes `j / k_store_block` with no cast; a size_t field initialized from a size_t expression. - [should-fix] tron-3 (tron) t/t_k_vnni_layout.cpp:794 No assertion in the page-state test can fail if k_forget_row keeps the saved-row bits of the offsets above the forgotten one.
  detail: Note [K VNNI storage] and k_forget_row (kv_cache.hpp lines 2566-2580) drop the row bits of the forgotten offset AND of every offset above it. That rule matters: if the bits above survived, the next single row saved into a block above (whose block bit was dropped but whose bytes were left in the VNNI form) would complete the block, and block_to_vnni would then read the other 15 rows as row-major bytes and scramble the block. I traced each REQUIRE of the case "page K blocks turn VNNI at the 16th saved row and back when a row is forgotten" against the broken variant (rows_below mask replaced by "only the offset's bit"): after the forget at 3 (rows 16..20 saved), re-saving 3..20 completes block 0 at 15 either way; after the forget at 18, block 1 completes at 31 either way (rows 21..30 are unsaved in both variants); after the forget at 16 and at the final forget at 3, the case ends with a bits check (vnni_blocks == 0) and a bytes check that both variants pass. So the "rows above lose their bits" rule has no discriminating check. One row saved again after the final forget discriminates: the correct code returns false (block 1 has one row), the broken variant returns true and converts. The addition runs in both builds (save_row_state returns false in the row-major build too). The scratch copy with this edit passes clang-format 19.1.7 --dry-run --Werror (rc 0); the old text occurs once at HEAD.
  fix: Exact old text (occurs once):
#ifdef TRON_K_VNNI

#endif

Exact new text:
#ifdef TRON_K_VNNI
  // Block 1's 4 panels are byte-identical to the snapshot.
  REQUIRE(block_1_bytes() == snap);
#endif
  // The rows of block 1 lost their bits with the forget at offset 3: one row
  // saved again does not complete the block.

  page->set_k_row(
      SLOT_0, 0, FORGOTTEN_TOKEN_16, tron::k_source_row<HEAD_SIZE_128>(row.data()));

  vote correctness: stands: Not refuted. The coverage gap is real, with one correction to the finding's wording.

Words used here: "block" = 16 page offsets of the K plane; "row bits" = k_rows_saved_ (one bit per offset); "block bit" = k_vnni_blocks_ (one bit per converted block).

What the code does. k_forget_row drops the row bits at and above the offset in every block (rows_below = offset_bit(offset) - 1) and the block bits of the offset's block and above [h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp:2554-2558, 2581-2582]. note_k_row_saved returns true whenever the block's 16 row bits are all set and its block bit is clear. It does not check that this row's bit was new [kv_cache.hpp:2169-2184, comment at 2161-2167]. k_block_to_vnni_at then converts the bytes in place when those two conditions hold [kv_cache.hpp:2216-2238].

Trace of the broken variant the finding's own steps describe (the offset's block loses its bits at and above the offset, but blocks above keep their row bits). Phases of the case [t/t_k_vnni_layout.cpp:654-795]: forget 3 with rows 0..20 saved → rows {0,1,2}, block 1 bits {16..20} survive; re-save 3..20 completes block 0 at 15 either way (line 728-729 holds). Forget 18 → bits 18..20 dropped in both variants. Forget 16 → bits 16..31 dropped in both. Re-save 16..31 → block 1 converted in both. Final forget 3 → correct: rows {0,1,2}; broken: rows {0,1,2,16..31}; blocks = 0 in both. The three closing checks (vnni_blocks == 0 at 788, check_rows(3) at 789, block_1_bytes() == snap at 792) pass in both. No REQUIRE discriminates.

No other test covers the rule. clear_kv_complete_at is called only from the scheduler [h/tron/scheduler/full.hpp:669, 818, 3346] and from t/t_k_vnni_layout.cpp; k_forget_row is private with that one caller [kv_cache.hpp:2478]. The other three cases in the file forget only inside block 0 with no converted block above (lines 853, 893; the append case never forgets). t_llama_unit.cpp:4045-4057 and t_amx_dispatch_dtype.cpp:135 use note_k_row_saved and k_vnni_blocks for save_k marks and the forward-end conversion, not the forget path.

The proposed fix discriminates. After the final forget at 3: correct code → row 16 is the only bit of block 1 → note_k_row_saved returns false, vnni_blocks stays 0. Broken variant → bit 16 already set, block 1 full, block bit clear → returns true, save_row_state converts, vnni_blocks becomes 0x2. Both new REQUIREs fail on the broken variant. Row-major build: note_k_row_saved returns BLOCK_COMPLETED_FALSE [kv_cache.hpp:2173-2176] and vnni_blocks returns NO_BLOCK_0X0 [t_k_vnni_layout.cpp:642-650], so the addition passes there too. The old text occurs exactly once (python count on HEAD = 1), and FORGOTTEN_TOKEN_16, SALT_5, NO_BLOCK_0X0, row, SLOT_0 are all in scope of the case.

Correction to the finding's wording. The variant it names literally ("rows_below mask replaced by only the offset's bit") IS caught by the existing test: after the forget at 3, bits 4..15 would survive, so the re-save of row 3 fills block 0 and note_k_row_saved returns true at tok 3, where line 728-729 expects false in the VNNI build. The undiscriminated rule is narrower: the row bits of the blocks ABOVE the forgotten offset's block must drop. The finding's trace already assumes that narrower variant; only its headline and the "only the offset's bit" phrase overstate it.
    better_fix: Keep the proposed addition, with the comment naming the exact rule and the hazard it guards, and with the finding's text narrowed to "the row bits of the blocks above the forgotten offset's block":

#ifdef TRON_K_VNNI
  // Block 1's 4 panels are byte-identical to the snapshot.
  REQUIRE(block_1_bytes() == snap);
#endif
  // The forget at offset 3 also dropped the row bits of block 1, the block
  // above: one row saved again does not complete it. Were the bits kept, the
  // row would complete the block and block_to_vnni would re-convert its VNNI
  // bytes as row-major rows.

  REQUIRE(vnni_blocks(page, SLOT_0) == NO_BLOCK_0X0);

So the converted-block return `k_row_view<...>{}` is an invalid view, and `REQUIRE_FALSE(...valid())` tests the documented contract. The insertion point is inside `#ifdef TRON_K_VNNI` with `plane` (`k_packed<GEOMETRY_1X128>`, a `k_vnni_view<const bf16,...>`) in scope [t_k_vnni_layout.cpp:697-717], after 21 saved rows with block 0 converted and block 1 row-major (asserted at lines 684-696). `page` is a non-const `page_t*`, matching the non-const accessor. `BLOCK_TOKENS_16` (file-scope using, line 40), `LAST_OF_BLOCK_0_15` (line 663), `SLOT_0`, `GEOMETRY_1X128` (anon namespace, ~line 617) are all in scope. Comparing `bf16*` (accessor's view) to `bf16 const*` (`row_major_row(plane, 16).data`) is a valid pointer comparison, and both point into the same kv_block panels, so the alias check is meaningful. - [must-fix] protocol-1 (protocol) h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp:2543 k_storage_readable tests the slot's first chunk, not the page's own chunk, so a converted block of a sliding-window slot can lose its bit without being transposed back and its live rows are then read as row-major garbage.
  detail: k_forget_row (kv_cache.hpp 2554-2592) converts a block back to row-major before dropping its bit only when k_storage_readable(storage_offset) is true. k_storage_readable (2541-2544) returns `book.reclaimable_kv_restore_attempt_ == 0 && book.slot_base(storage_offset) != nullptr`. For a reclaimable (sliding-window) slot, slot_base (1568-1580) answers for chunk 0 only: `reclaimable_chunks_.front().data`. A sliding-window slot keeps its pages in several chunks (initialize_reclaimable_chunks, 1583-1600; slot_page 1669-1676 picks the chunk by page number), and reclaim_out_of_window_kv (full.hpp 3380-3402) frees the oldest chunks first while the tail chunk stays resident.

Interleaving (all under the tree lock on the worker, between forwards, so no race is needed): (1) a prefix node P continues in book B; a child C continues B (kv_location_for_tokens, full.hpp 1017-1033: the parent's book when the node is a leaf); C's rows complete block c that straddles P's end; the forward end converts block c (bit set). (2) Under memory pressure the reclaim frees B's chunk 0 (free_reclaimable_kv_chunk resets chunk.data) while the tail chunk k >= 1 that holds page of block c stays resident. (3) C is released: ~parent_edge decrements B's token count to P's end (token_tree.hpp 166), the bits stay. (4) A new child continues B at P's end: create_child_at_location (full.hpp 669) -> clear_kv_complete_at(offset) -> k_forget_row: block_keeps_rows is true (offset % 16 != 0), converted has bit c, but k_storage_readable sees chunk 0's null data and returns false. The bits are dropped without block_to_rows. (5) The next forward reads P's rows below the offset in block c: get_k_row / k_row_if_row_major / the software loop see a clear block bit and read row_major_row positions that hold VNNI-packed bytes. Wrong K values for up to 15 rows of that sliding-window layer, silently (no assert).

The opposite mismatch (chunk 0 resident, the page's chunk reclaimed, readable answers true and kv_block_at_storage asserts "Access to missing KV slot") is not reachable: reserve_book_tokens (full.hpp 982-1010) makes the new offset's chunk resident before clear_kv_complete_at runs, and invalidate_restored_book runs with attempt != 0, which short-circuits before slot_base. The restore path is safe: the commit (src/tron/scheduler/full.cpp 128-176) invalidates every entry of the restored chain, so every row is replayed and rewritten row-major.

Trigger conditions: a 128-dimension-head model whose generated plugin declares a sliding-window K slot (ingest/src/TronCpp.hs 545 emits tron::kv_slot_retention::sliding_window), a shared prefix longer than one chunk, memory pressure that reclaimed chunk 0, a child dropped and the prefix re-extended at a mid-block offset. Insufficient data on which shipped model has both 128-dimension heads and sliding-window layers (gpt-oss has 64-dimension heads; gemma has 256); the code path does not depend on a model name. The existing tests use uniform_kv_cache books with retained slots only, so none of them reaches a chunked slot.

The fix uses the page-local accessor that kv_block_at_storage itself uses (slot_page, 1669-1676; page is a friend of book, 1416-1417, and k_storage_readable already reads the private reclaimable_kv_restore_attempt_). slot_page's division by reclaimable_chunk_pages_ is safe: a reclaimable storage offset implies reclaimable_kv_bytes_ != 0, so initialize_reclaimable_chunks set the page count and built one chunk entry per page range; a retained offset takes the slot_base path as before. The new text compiles in every configuration (no layout_on dependence). A test for the fix (report only, not an exact-text edit): a fixed_kv_layout book with one sliding-window slot and at least two chunks, rows of a page in chunk 1 saved and converted, free_reclaimable_kv_chunk(0), then clear_kv_complete_at at a mid-block offset of that page; the rows below the offset must still read back through get_k_row.
  fix: Exact old text (occurs once at HEAD, h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp lines 2536-2544):

  // Whether the bytes of the storage slot may be read and written now: the
  // book's storage is published (no private KV restore in flight) and the
  // slot's arena is allocated. clear_kv_complete_at runs on both sides of a
  // KV restore commit (full.hpp invalidate_restored_book).
  TRON(nodiscard, inline)
  bool k_storage_readable(kv_storage_offset storage_offset) const noexcept {
    return book.reclaimable_kv_restore_attempt_ == 0 &&
           book.slot_base(storage_offset) != nullptr;
  }

Exact new text:

  // Whether the bytes of this page's storage slot may be read and written
  // now: the book's storage is published (no private KV restore in flight)
  // and the page's storage is resident. A sliding-window slot keeps its pages
  // in chunks that are reclaimed one by one (free_reclaimable_kv_chunk), so
  // the page's own chunk decides, not the slot's first chunk.
  // clear_kv_complete_at runs on both sides of a KV restore commit (full.hpp
  // invalidate_restored_book).
  TRON(nodiscard, inline)
  bool k_storage_readable(kv_storage_offset storage_offset) const noexcept {
    return book.reclaimable_kv_restore_attempt_ == 0 &&
           book.slot_page(storage_offset, pageno()).base != nullptr;
  }
  vote correctness: stands: Short version: every factual claim in the finding matches the code at HEAD 144a6dedd3 (worktree /home/jhan/workspace/ai-runs/tron-split-pr4). The readability test in k_forget_row asks about the slot's first chunk, while the page's bytes live in the page's own chunk. No other code handles the mismatch, so the finding stands.

Words used here: "book" is one KV (key/value cache) allocation; "chunk" is one DMA (direct memory access) arena holding a range of a sliding-window slot's pages; "block" is a 16-token group inside a 64-token page; "VNNI" is the packed K layout used by the AMX kernel; "converted bit" is the per-block flag that says the block holds the VNNI layout.

Evidence, step by step:

- k_storage_readable returns `book.reclaimable_kv_restore_attempt_ == 0 && book.slot_base(storage_offset) != nullptr` [h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp:2541-2544]. k_forget_row runs block_to_rows only when that returns true, then unconditionally drops the block and row bits [kv_cache.hpp:2573-2586].
- slot_base reads `reclaimable_chunks_.front().data` for a reclaimable slot, so it answers for chunk 0 only [kv_cache.hpp:1568-1580]. slot_page picks the chunk by page number (`pg / reclaimable_chunk_pages_`) [kv_cache.hpp:1669-1676]. Both kv_block [kv_cache.hpp:1757-1760] and kv_block_at_storage [kv_cache.hpp:1797-1800] read pages through slot_page, so reads of a page in a resident tail chunk succeed while chunk 0 is freed.
- free_reclaimable_kv_chunk resets one chunk's data and marks it reclaimed [kv_cache.hpp:1177-1192]. scan_reclamation_boundary walks chunks from 0 upward and skips only chunks whose newest token is within the margin of the tip's query position [h/tron/scheduler/full.hpp:3380-3402]. So chunk 0 is freed first while the tail chunk stays resident.
- A book can have more than one chunk: the chunk target is 256 MiB [kv_cache.hpp:1588] and the per-book DMA cap is the 1 GiB hugepage size [src/system/memory.cpp:472-476, h/system/heap_v2.hpp:56], so a book may hold up to 4 chunks of reclaimable storage.
- The released child leaves its parent a leaf: ~node erases itself from the parent's children map [full.hpp:866-890]. kv_location_for_tokens then continues the parent's book when `children.empty()` [full.hpp:1017-1033]. ~parent_edge only decrements the token count; no bit is cleared [h/tron/scheduler/token_tree.hpp:166-168].
- The new child's path: create_child reserves the new offsets (reserve_book_tokens, which allocates only the chunks of the new range [kv_cache.hpp:1205-1245]) and then create_child_at_location calls clear_kv_complete_at for each new entry [full.hpp:716-724, 669]. clear_kv_complete_at calls k_forget_row [kv_cache.hpp:2474-2479]. With chunk 0 freed and the page in a resident tail chunk, k_storage_readable is false, so the converted bit is dropped without block_to_rows.
- No restore rescues this case. prepare_reclaimed_kv_restore restores a reclaimed chunk only when the demand position minus the chunk's newest token is below the margin (window + 1 + 64 tokens) [full.hpp:3360-3368, 1699-1704, 3256-3276]. A chunk 0 far enough behind the prefix end stays reclaimed and no invalidation runs.
- Readers take the layout from the bit: get_k_row [kv_cache.hpp:2293-2309] and k_row_if_row_major [kv_cache.hpp:2343-2352] read row-major when the bit is clear. The bytes of a converted block are VNNI-packed, so rows below the new offset in that block read back wrong. No assert fires.
- Scope: layout_on is true only for head size 128 [h/tron/tensor/k_vnni.hpp:126], so a sliding-window slot with 128-dimension heads is required. The plugin emitter declares sliding-window retention from the model's attention type [ingest/src/TronCpp.hs:539-545], so the path exists for any such model. I found no shipped model in the repo that has both (insufficient data: the model JSON configs are not in this checkout).
- The Note itself encodes the wrong assumption: it says that for "reclaimed storage" the bytes "are not valid anyway" [kv_cache.hpp:712-717]. That holds per book, but reclamation is per chunk [kv_cache.hpp:1177-1178 comment].
- Tests: the one chunked sliding-window test ("Sliding KV chunks reserve and restore transactionally") writes rows with set_k_row, frees and restores chunks, and reads back with get_k_row [t/t_llama_unit.cpp:747-990]. It never converts a block and never calls clear_kv_complete_at, so it does not cover this path. t/t_k_vnni_layout.cpp uses uniform_kv_cache books with full-history slots only [t/t_k_vnni_layout.cpp:490, 625, 845].

Proposed fix check: `book.slot_page(storage_offset, pageno()).base != nullptr` is the same accessor kv_block_at_storage uses, page is a friend of book [kv_cache.hpp:1557], and reclaimable_chunks_ is never empty for a live book with a reclaimable slot (initialize_reclaimable_chunks pushes at least one chunk when reclaimable_kv_bytes_ != 0 [kv_cache.hpp:1584-1601]; clear_kv_storage runs only on construction failure, after which try_create returns nullptr [kv_cache.hpp:1464-1476]). The fix is sound.

1. Old text present exactly once. k_storage_readable is defined once [h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp:2541] and used once [kv_cache.hpp:2574]. The comment phrase "slot's arena is allocated" occurs once (grep -c = 1). Lines 2536-2544 match the quoted old text byte for byte (checked with cat -A: no trailing whitespace, 2-space indent).

2. New text is valid C++ with the needed access. book::slot_page(kv_storage_offset, size_t) is const noexcept and returns storage_page{uint8_t* base; size_t pages; size_t page;} [kv_cache.hpp:1662-1677]. page::pageno() is const noexcept [kv_cache.hpp:2628]. page is a friend of book [kv_cache.hpp:1417], so the private slot_page and nested storage_page are reachable, the same way the old code reached the private slot_base and reclaimable_kv_restore_attempt_. k_storage_readable stays const noexcept; slot_page is noexcept.

3. The new check is the one kv_block_at_storage itself performs. kv_block_at_storage calls slot_page(offset, pg) and asserts storage.base != nullptr "Access to missing KV slot" [kv_cache.hpp:1797-1798]. So after the fix, readable == true implies block_to_rows will not hit that assert, and readable == false exactly when the page's own bytes are absent. The old slot_base answers for reclaimable_chunks_.front() only [kv_cache.hpp:1571-1576].

4. The finding's premise holds. scan_reclamation_boundary frees chunks in index order 0 upward and skips chunks whose newest token is inside the margin, so chunk 0 can be freed while a later chunk stays resident [h/tron/scheduler/full.hpp:3380-3402]; free_reclaimable_kv_chunk resets only that chunk's data [kv_cache.hpp:1177-1192]. reserve_token_range checks only the chunks in the requested range, so a reclaimed chunk 0 does not block continuing the book in a resident tail chunk [kv_cache.hpp:1209-1216]. create_child_at_location then calls clear_kv_complete_at on the new offsets [full.hpp:669]. With the old check, the block bit is dropped without block_to_rows and the live rows are misread.

5. No division-by-zero or new assert path. slot_page divides by reclaimable_chunk_pages_ only for a reclaimable location [kv_cache.hpp:1671-1672]. A reclaimable location means a slot with retention.reclaimable() and a nonzero geometry (static_assert "nonzero, SIMD-aligned geometries" [kv_cache.hpp:870]), so reclaimable_kv_bytes_ = n_pages * unit > 0 whenever n_pages > 0, and initialize_reclaimable_chunks then sets reclaimable_chunk_pages_ >= 1 [kv_cache.hpp:1584-1600]. When n_pages == 0 no page exists, so k_forget_row never runs. clear_kv_storage (which empties the chunk vector) is called only on constructor failure [kv_cache.hpp:1468,1474], and such a book is never published (try_create returns nullptr, public constructor asserts storage_valid_ [kv_cache.hpp:1031,1433]).

6. Behavior is unchanged where the old code was right. For a retained slot (every uniform_kv_cache book in t/t_k_vnni_layout.cpp, which has no sliding_window/fixed_kv_layout/free_reclaimable_kv_chunk use, grep count 0) slot_page returns {slot_base(offset), n_pages, pg}, so .base equals the old slot_base result. The restore path short-circuits on reclaimable_kv_restore_attempt_ != 0 before slot_page, as before. The expression has no dependence on TRON_K_VNNI (k_vnni::layout_on, h/tron/tensor/k_vnni.hpp:124-129) or TRON_AMX_DISPATCH (CMakeLists.txt:46), and k_forget_row is compiled in all four combinations; with TRON_K_VNNI off no block bit is ever set (k_block_to_vnni_at converts only under layout_on [kv_cache.hpp:2228-2236]), so the branch is dead and the fix is inert.

7. The new comment is truthful: chunk.data non-null with attempt == 0 means state resident (reserve_token_range sets resident after allocating [kv_cache.hpp:1239-1240]; abort resets data [kv_cache.hpp:1286]; finish sets resident [kv_cache.hpp:1309]). "Reclaimed one by one (free_reclaimable_kv_chunk)" matches the per-chunk loop in full.hpp. The sentence about invalidate_restored_book is kept.

The "report only" test suggestion is right to leave as a report: a fixed_kv_layout book with a sliding-window slot, two chunks, free_reclaimable_kv_chunk(0) then clear_kv_complete_at at a mid-block offset is a new test file section, not an exact-text edit, and the lead can add it separately. A senior tron reviewer would accept the one-line expression change as-is; the only thing they might ask for in addition is that test.

(1) h/tron/tensor/k_vnni.hpp, old text:
// Copy one token between packed K planes (or within one plane): the token's
// row is gathered into a 64-byte-aligned local row (load_row) and scattered
// into its destination column (store_row). Tokens own disjoint slots, so no
// other token of the destination is touched. Both token indices are checked by
// those two operations.
template <size_t Rows, size_t Cols>
TRON(inline)
void copy_token(k_vnni_view<bf16, Rows, Cols> dst,
    size_t dst_token,
    k_vnni_view<const bf16, Rows, Cols> src,
    size_t src_token) noexcept {
  alignas(64) bf16 row[Cols];
  load_row(src, src_token, k_destination_row<Cols>(row));
  store_row(dst, dst_token, k_source_row<Cols>(row));
}

// Note [Row-major tail block]
new text:
// Note [Row-major tail block]

- [should-fix] protocol-2 (protocol) h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp:698 Note [K VNNI storage] claims the attention workers never read a block while a later minibatch saves it; the earlier minibatch's workers do, because the dependency latch only waits for upstream minibatches.
  detail: The Note (kv_cache.hpp 693-701) says: "The attention workers themselves are not that reader: a pending page is read only after every minibatch that writes it counted its latch down." That is not what the dependency tracking does. finalize_minibatches (model.hpp, the loop that fills downstream_minibatches and kvs_needed) makes other_q_batch wait for q_batch only when other_q_batch's queries need one of q_batch's tokens (token_ix_needed). With a block split across minibatches A (rows 0..7, earlier tokens) and B (rows 8..15, later tokens), A does not depend on B (causal attention), so A's attention workers read block c's rows 0..7 row-major (row_major_row through the dotters, or qk_group when the block were VNNI) while main runs B's save_k into rows 8..15 of the same block. With the row-major store this is byte-disjoint and safe, and it is a second reason why the conversion cannot run inside save_k: converting block c in B's save_k would transpose rows 0..7 under A's workers. The protocol as implemented (conversion after run() joined the workers and after wait_for_coop_gof) is correct; the sentence that justifies it is false, and a reviewer who checks the forward-end item of the handoff (step 5.2) reads this paragraph first. Only a comment changes, so the three configurations are unaffected; the referenced Note [Minibatch Dependency Tracking] exists in h/tron/models/common.hpp:966, so lint-notes passes.
  fix: Exact old text (occurs once at HEAD, h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp lines 693-701):

//     not in save_k: a block may be split across two minibatches of one pass
//     (grains of 8 tokens), and the cooperative GOF staging gather of the
//     earlier minibatch's tokens (help_drain_gof on the attention workers,
//     wait_for_coop_gof on main) reads the block's rows row-major while the
//     later minibatch saves the block's last rows. The attention workers
//     themselves are not that reader: a pending page is read only after every
//     minibatch that writes it counted its latch down. The forward end, after
//     wait_for_coop_gof, is the first point where no reader of this pass is
//     left. The in-place transposes of clear_kv_complete_at and copy_from rely

Exact new text:

//     not in save_k: a block may be split across two minibatches of one pass
//     (grains of 8 tokens), and two readers of this pass hold the block's
//     rows row-major while the later minibatch saves the block's last rows.
//     One is the attention of the earlier minibatch: its queries do not
//     depend on the later tokens (Note [Minibatch Dependency Tracking] in
//     common.hpp), so its workers can read the page while the later
//     minibatch's save_k runs. The other is the cooperative GOF staging
//     gather of the earlier minibatch's tokens (help_drain_gof on the
//     attention workers, wait_for_coop_gof on main). The forward end, after
//     run() joined the workers and wait_for_coop_gof drained the gather, is
//     the first point where no reader of this pass is left. The in-place
//     transposes of clear_kv_complete_at and copy_from rely
  vote correctness: REFUTED: The finding's premise is wrong: it says the dependency latch makes A wait for B "only when A's queries need one of B's tokens", so a causal earlier minibatch A never waits for a later minibatch B that shares a block. The code's check is page-granular, not token-granular, so A does wait for B.

Evidence, all at HEAD 144a6dedd3 in /home/jhan/workspace/ai-runs/tron-split-pr4:

1. token_ix_needed is a page-wide check [h/tron/models/common.hpp:1068-1082]. It takes the producer token's page and walks every mask range over [page.at_offset(0), page.count()). It returns true when any range on that page is visible to any query of the consumer batch (range.any_visible(items_mask)). The comment at common.hpp:1069-1072 states the intent: "Wait for every producer of a page that any query sees, even when the producer's own token is invisible."

2. A split 16-token block lies inside one page (block c = page offsets 16c..16c+15, kv_cache.hpp:677-679). A's rows 0..7 and B's rows 8..15 are on the same page. The range that covers A's own tokens carries A's queries: build_edge_ranges closes the range above a query before inserting that query into the mask, then emits the range that covers the query's own token with the query included [src/tron/models/ranged_mask.cpp:46-50, 64-66, 84]. A token sees itself. So for the shared page, any_visible(A.items_mask) is true.

3. finalize_minibatches runs the double loop over all ordered pairs [h/tron/models/model.hpp:2012-2025]. With q_batch = B and other_q_batch = A, B's first item on the shared page makes A->token_ix_needed(...) return true, so A.kvs_needed += 2 and A is pushed onto B.downstream_minibatches. A's latch for the slot is reset to kvs_needed [model.hpp:2033-2038].

4. B's save_k_impl counts A's latch down only at its end, after every row store and after the shared-window join [model.hpp:3453-3455]; save_v_impl likewise [model.hpp:3529-3531].

5. A's attention workers read a pending page only in the pending sections: run_attention_job runs run_sections(pending=false) first, then waits on outer_batch->upstream_kvs_ready[binding.kv_slot].wait(), then run_sections(pending=true) [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:813-829]; run_sections selects pending_kv_plan by the flag [self_attention.hpp:1165-1166]. The pending page set is global over every do_kv token job of the pass [model.hpp:1927-1933], so the shared page is pending for A.

So A's workers cannot read block c while B's save_k writes rows 8..15: A's pending read waits for B's save_k and save_v on that slot. The Note's sentence "a pending page is read only after every minibatch that writes it counted its latch down" [kv_cache.hpp:697-699] matches the code. The latch is per slot, and a slot is per layer/operation [common.hpp:1159-1162], so a cross-layer read/write pair touches different K planes and is not a counterexample either.

The proposed replacement text would insert a false claim ("its queries do not depend on the later tokens ... so its workers can read the page while the later minibatch's save_k runs"), and it would also lose the correct statement that the GOF staging gather is the only reader left.
    better_fix: No change needed. Optional precision only: the sentence at kv_cache.hpp:697-699 could add the reason in one clause, e.g. "... counted its latch down (token_ix_needed in common.hpp is page-wide: a minibatch waits for every writer of a page that any of its queries sees)". The current text is correct as written.
  vote fixq: REFUTED: Short version: the proposed fix replaces a true sentence with a false one. The dependency tracking is page-wide, not per token, so the earlier minibatch A does wait for the later minibatch B when both write the same page. The existing Note text is correct and should stay.

Mechanics of the fix (these parts are fine): the old text occurs exactly once at HEAD 144a6dedd3, h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp line 693 (python count = 1). Only a comment changes, so TRON_K_VNNI on/off and TRON_AMX_DISPATCH on/off compile and behave the same. Note [Minibatch Dependency Tracking] exists at h/tron/models/common.hpp:966.

Why the new prose is wrong:
- token_ix_needed(tok, state) decides per page, not per token. It takes page_of(tok) and walks every mask range of that page, and returns true when any query of the consumer minibatch sees any range of that page [h/tron/models/common.hpp:1068-1082]. Its own comment says: "Wait for every producer of a page that any query sees, even when the producer's own token is invisible" [common.hpp:1069-1072].
- finalize_minibatches records consumer other_q_batch as downstream of producer q_batch when token_ix_needed is true for any producer token, and its comment says "A failed page-wide check covers the remaining consecutive producer tokens on the same physical page" [h/tron/models/model.hpp:2012-2026].
- In the finding's scenario, A (rows 0..7 of block c) and B (rows 8..15) write the same page P (a 16-token block lies inside one 64-token page). A's queries see A's own tokens on P (a token attends to itself, Note [Ready versus pending attention work], model.hpp:1943-1947). So token_ix_needed(B's token, A) is true, A->kvs_needed += 2, and A is in B->downstream_minibatches. B's save_k and save_v count A's latch down [model.hpp:3454-3455, 3530-3531].
- Every page that any KV-writing token of this pass touches is a "pending" page [model.hpp:1928-1935]. A's attention reads pending pages only in the pending run_sections call, which comes after outer_batch->upstream_kvs_ready[slot].wait() [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:821-827]. The ready plan never contains page P.
- So A's attention workers read block c only after B's save_k finished. The sentence the finding calls false ("a pending page is read only after every minibatch that writes it counted its latch down") matches the implementation. The replacement sentence ("its queries do not depend on the later tokens ... so its workers can read the page while the later minibatch's save_k runs") contradicts common.hpp:1069-1072 and model.hpp:2016-2017.
- Corner case checked: if no query of A sees any range of page P, A does not wait for B, but then apply_page_range skips P entirely (per-query "if (!sees_page) continue", self_attention.hpp:1597-1611; the AMX fast path packs Q only after the same visibility check, self_attention.hpp:1531-1536). No reader conflict exists in that case either.

A senior tron reviewer would reject the fix: it would make the Note disagree with token_ix_needed and the dependency comments, and a later reader might then think a conversion inside save_k is unsafe for a reason that does not exist, while the real reason (the GOF staging gather) is already in the Note.
    better_fix: No edit: keep the Note text at h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp:693-701 as it is at HEAD. The finding should be closed as "not a defect" with the page-wide rule as the reason (common.hpp:1068-1082, model.hpp:2016-2026).

  vote scope: REFUTED: Rule: no demonstrated defect (rule 1's condition, and rule 6: a should-fix label without a defect). The sentence the finding calls false is true as written, so the proposed rewrite would replace a correct claim with an incorrect one.

The finding's premise is "A does not depend on B (causal attention)". The dependency tracking is per page, not per token. token_ix_needed (h/tron/models/common.hpp:1068-1082) walks every range on the page of the producer's token and returns true when any range on that page is visible to any query of the consumer minibatch. Its own comment states the rule: "Wait for every producer of a page that any query sees, even when the producer's own token is invisible." In the split-block case, A (rows 0..7) and B (rows 8..15) write the same page. A's queries see A's own tokens on that page (a token attends to itself, Note [Ready versus pending attention work], model.hpp:1938-1957). So token_ix_needed(B's token, A) is true. finalize_minibatches (model.hpp:2013-2025) then adds A to B's downstream_minibatches and raises A's kvs_needed by 2, and resets A's upstream_kvs_ready[slot] latch to that count (model.hpp:2033-2038).

That page is a pending page for A, because B's tokens do KV work on it (model.hpp:1927-1935). A's attention workers read pending pages only in the second run_sections call, which comes after outer_batch->upstream_kvs_ready[slot].wait() (self_attention.hpp:821-827). B's save_k counts that latch down only after every row of B is stored (model.hpp:3453-3455; the shared-save join happens-before the count_down per Note [Shared block save], model.hpp:3136-3141). So A's workers cannot read rows 0..7 of block c while B's save_k writes rows 8..15. The Note's sentence "a pending page is read only after every minibatch that writes it counted its latch down" matches the code.

Scope checks: the nine lines are added lines of the PR-4 diff (git diff jhan-vnnik-3-shared-save HEAD -- h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp shows them as '+'), so rule 4 does not apply. The change is comment-only, so rules 2, 3 and 7 do not apply. The new text's Note cross-reference is not narration (rule 5). The refutation rests on the false premise, not on scope.
    better_fix: None required. The current text is correct. If the author wants the page-granular rule visible at the reading point, an optional clause suffices without changing the claim: after "counted its latch down" add "(token_ix_needed in common.hpp waits for every writer of a page any query sees, so the earlier minibatch waits for the later one too)". This is a nit, not a should-fix.

- [should-fix] plain-16 (plain) /home/jhan/workspace/intel-AMX/exec/pr4-20261012/msgs/a.txt:53 a.txt paragraph 3: save_k, 'operation' and 'decode' used before any definition, and a ', so' consequence.

  fix: OLD:
Note [Row-major blocks under hardware attention]: when the FPGA scores
an operation's K (cached_operation_uses_hw in model.hpp), save_k
records no row bits for the rows of a short run and queues no block. A
block completed by short runs then stays row-major. A whole-block save
still writes the VNNI layout. Past the engagement point (the first
sequence position the FPGA scores) the FPGA holds the K of every
complete GOF (group of four consecutive tokens) and the CPU scores only
the tail above them, so the AMX kernel never reads a block that decode
completes there.
NEW:
Note [Row-major blocks under hardware attention]: when the FPGA scores
the K of an operation (one attention operation of the model, one per
layer, asked through cached_operation_uses_hw in model.hpp), save_k
(the function of h/tron/models/model.hpp that stores one pass's K
rows) records no row bits for the rows of a short run and queues no
block. A block completed by short runs then stays row-major. A
whole-block save still writes the VNNI layout. Past the engagement
point (the first sequence position the FPGA scores) the FPGA holds the
K of every complete GOF (group of four consecutive tokens) and the CPU
scores only the tail above them. So the AMX kernel never reads a block
that decode (the passes after the prompt, one token per user per pass)
